(1) EgyptERA tariff 'starting from April 2026' raised only the >1,000 kWh/month household band, 223 → 258 pt/kWh (+16 %), holding 0-50 / 51-100 / 101-200 / 201-350 / 351-650 / 651-1,000 kWh at 68 / 78 / 95 / 155 / 195 / 210 pt; industrial and commercial tariffs rose 17-31 %, the lowest commercial bracket (under 100 kWh) +91 % (EgyptERA, 2026 data; IMF CR 26/224, 17 Jul 2026). (2) On Fri 31 Jul 2026 the Ministry of Electricity and Renewable Energy announced an average +12 % on every household band above 50 kWh, 0-50 kWh held at 68 pt, the state bearing ~EGP 100bn/yr (ministry figure); press reports place it from July 2026 consumption, first billed in August 2026 (press only — Enterprise 2 Aug 2026, Ahram Online). Per-band rates UNVERIFIED: outlets disagree (>1,000 kWh at 289 pt per Enterprise vs 274 pt per Al-Masry Al-Youm via Egypt Independent) and on 2026-10-28 EgyptERA still lists the April 2026 table as current ('Current Tariff From April 2026') — quote band rates only once EgyptERA posts the new table. On 10 Oct 2026 the minister said the consumption brackets are under review (Ahram Online, 11 Oct 2026, secondary); no decision as of 2026-10-28 (Ministry of Electricity home-page news, latest item 13 Oct 2026). SUPERSEDES the 'freeze through Jun 2026' wording in shared/mains-reliability.md | EgyptERA April 2026 tariff https://egyptera.org/en/TarrifApril2026.aspx + IMF Country Report 26/224 https://www.imf.org/-/media/files/publications/cr/2026/english/1egyea2026002.pdf + Enterprise 2 Aug 2026 (secondary) https://enterpriseam.com/egypt/2026/08/02/household-electricity-tariffs-hiked-by-an-average-of-12 + Ahram Online (secondary) https://english.ahram.org.eg/News/573852.aspx | 2026-10-28 | 2026-12-01 | 3 | --mains`

WITHDRAWN 2: 'without it future USD repatriation of sale proceeds structurally blocked, sellers stuck with EGP-only exit' — no primary source states this, and the nearest primary statement points the other way: US State Dept ICS 2025 Egypt (Remittance Policies) 'The 1992 U.S.-Egypt Bilateral Investment Treaty provides for free transfer of dividends, royalties, compensation for expropriation, payments arising out of an investment dispute, contract payments, and proceeds from sales' (a US-investor treaty right, not a general rule for all nationalities). RE-ATTRIBUTED from CBE to the Ministry of Justice (Real Estate Publicity Department), which GAFI names as the 'Relevant government agency'. ESTABLISHED ONLY AS AT 13 Nov 2024, the Last-Modified date of the GAFI FDI Regulations PDF (header re-checked 2026-08-07): 'A law is currently under review to amend certain provisions of Law No. 230 of 1996. The amendment aims to allow non-Egyptians to own an unlimited number of properties in Egypt, eliminating the previous restriction of two properties in two different cities, provided payment is made in foreign currency'; the same page records that the Council of Ministers approved the amendment 'in session No. 246 on July 5, 2023' and that 'The Ministry of Justice has completed the drafting of this amendment to be presented to the Egyptian Parliament'. - `ongoing (Law 14/2012; Sharm El Sheikh / Dahab / Gulf of Aqaba regime in force 2022-04-01) | foreign-buyer | Sinai — no freehold for foreigners, usufruct only, under two regimes. (1) Most of Sinai, Law 14/2012 on Integrated Development of Sinai (MIFT consolidated text): with approval of the National Authority for the Development of Sinai board plus Defence, Interior and General Intelligence, non-Egyptians may own built structures only, never the land, and may hold usufruct of residential units for at most 50 years (Art. 2); usufruct contracts run up to 50 years, renewable to a 75-year total with board approval, and never convert to ownership (Art. 3); military-strategic areas, border-adjacent areas (Presidential Decree 444/2014), Red Sea islands, nature reserves and antiquities sites are closed to any disposal; contracts without the approvals are absolutely void. (2) CORRECTION for Sharm El Sheikh, Dahab and the Gulf of Aqaba tourism sector: Presidential Decree 128/2022 (issued 23 Mar 2022, in force 1 Apr 2022) takes them out of Law 14/2012 — ownership of land AND built real estate is reserved to Egyptian nationals and wholly Egyptian-owned companies; state land is granted by usufruct of up to 75 years in total (not 50), the grantee owning the buildings it erects during the term, and buildings may be sold without the land; every residential allocation, usufruct grant or disposal, for Egyptians or foreigners, needs Defence, Interior and General Intelligence approval or the contract is absolutely void; non-Egyptian investment projects must be Egyptian joint-stock companies. The granting body is the competent land authority (the decree says جهات الولاية and does not name TDA) — the prior wording, leasehold from TDA, is not established. No source quantifies how often sales offices market Sinai units as freehold; skill guidance: treat any freehold label on a Sinai unit offered to a foreigner as a deal-killer red flag until the contract type and the security approvals are seen | Law 14/2012 consolidated text (MIFT) https://mift.gov.eg/legislation/download/299 + Presidential Decree 128/2022, Government Printing Office text http://alamiria.com/Sec/TashTxt?id=37B2gztNoYs%3D | 2026-10-21 | 2027-01-19 | 1 | --tax ownership`

- `2026-08-13 (Act 13 of 2026 s.15, in force on Gazette publication; item-6 Technical OP by Proclamation) | visa | Occupation Permit criteria RESET and Family Occupation Permit ABOLISHED: the Economic and Financial Measures (Miscellaneous Provisions) Act 2026 (Act No. 13 of 2026, dated 13 Aug 2026 on lawsofmauritius.govmu.org; Bill No. XIII of 2026 of 24 Jul 2026) s.15(e) replaces Part I of the EDB Act First Schedule (OP criteria) and repeals Part II (Family Occupation Permit, until then a USD 250,000 contribution to the COVID-19 Projects Development Fund); s.61 defers only the new Technical category (item 6) to a Proclamation. - `2025-07-01 | tax | PIT scale 0/10/20% took effect for the income year commencing 1 Jul 2025 (Finance Act 2025) and is still the scale MRA administers as at 2026-08-07 — the MRA PAYE table reads First Rs 500,000 at 0%, Next Rs 500,000 at 10%, Remainder 20%, and employees with monthly emoluments not exceeding Rs 38,462 are not subject to PAYE (company director and board fees excepted). CORRECTION to this entry's prior wording: the scale did NOT replace a "15% flat" rate. MRA's own Budget 2025/26 Fiscal Measures Highlights prints the outgoing scale beside the new one as ELEVEN bands — First Rs 390,000 at 0%, then 2%, 4%, 6%, 8%, 10%, 12%, 14%, 16%, 18%, remainder 20% — and states "The new income tax rates and bands will be effective as from income year starting on 01st July 2025". That eleven-band scale is the FA-2023 one effective from 1 Jul 2023, whose MRA communique of 08 Jun 2023 also records "The solidarity levy has been abolished". Budget Speech 2025-2026 para 261: "Regarding personal income tax, we are reducing the tax bands from eleven to three". ALSO LIVE for income year 2025-26 only: Fair Share Contribution — MRA states an individual pays FSC "in addition to his liability to income tax where his income threshold exceeds Rs 12 million in an income year", collected under PAYE "as from 01 July 2025 ... up to 30 June 2028", the employer withholding 15% of monthly emoluments exceeding Rs 923,077 (Rs 12,000,000 divided by 13), so the top marginal load for income year 2025-26 exceeds 20%. SUPERSEDED from income year 2026-27: Finance Act 2026 (Act 14 of 2026, gazetted 13 Aug 2026) enacted the Budget Speech 2026-2027 para 280 restructure — s.7(v) four-band scale 0/10/20/35% (35% above Rs 12 million) and s.7(b) limiting the individual FSC to income year 2025-26; see the 2026-07-01 PIT entry. - `2024-12-13 | foreign-buyer | 85/15 currency rule IN FORCE since 13 Dec 2024 (the Feb 2025 date is only the EDB FAQ explainer's own stamp, not the amendments' date). EDB (Smart City Scheme / Property Development Scheme / Invest Hotel Scheme / Real Estate Development Scheme) (Amendment No. 2) Regulations 2024 (Cabinet took note 6 Dec 2024, in force 13 Dec 2024) require a non-citizen acquiring residential property under IRS/RES/IHS/PDS/SCS, after transferring funds from abroad in hard convertible currency, to pay 85% of the purchase price to the promoter in Mauritius rupee with the remaining 15% in USD/EUR/any hard convertible FX or MUR; the notary receives the price in FX and effects the 85/15 split on transfer to the promoter (the bank applies the exchange rate on the transfer date instructed by notary and promoter, FAQ Q9); registration duty is payable in USD or other hard convertible FX (FAQ Q10). Local-loan floor raised USD 500,000 → USD 750,000: up to USD 750,000 the acquisition is financed from own funds transferred from abroad; where the price exceeds USD 750,000 the first USD 750,000 must be own funds transferred to Mauritius and paid in MUR to the promoter, with a Mauritian bank loan (may be contracted in MUR) only for the balance and loan repayment in hard convertible FX. Per EDB FAQ the amendments apply to a first sale only (resales out of scope) and NOT to Ground+2 apartments. Transition (FAQ Q5): the 85/15 rule applies to sales deeds signed from 13 Dec 2024 and does NOT apply to deeds signed before 13 Dec 2024 even where VEFA tranche payments fall due on or after that date; the USD 750,000 loan rule applies to EDB letters of authorisation (LOAs) issued from 13 Dec 2024 and to valid earlier LOAs where the sales deed was not yet signed. Resident carve-out (FAQ Q13/Q16/Q17): a non-citizen holding an Occupation Permit / retired or other Residence Permit may use funds previously transferred from abroad (on proof of transfer, subject to conditions), and one earning MUR income in Mauritius may finance the whole acquisition in MUR; residents may still borrow locally only above the first USD 750,000 of own funds. No repealing or amending regulation found (EDB newsroom notice 20 Dec 2024 + FAQ still published 2026-10-21; Finance Act 2026 (Act 14) and Economic and Financial Measures (Miscellaneous Provisions) Act 2026 (Act 13) contain no change to the schemes' payment rules). NB the 85% forced-MUR leg imposes a real FX-spread cost on the full purchase price for buyers funding from abroad. | EDB Mauritius (FAQ-Amendments document Feb 2025 + newsroom notice 20 Dec 2024) + https://edbmauritius.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/FAQ-Amendments-to-Property-Regulations.pdf + https://edbmauritius.org/newsroom/amendments-to-irs-res-ihs-pds-and-scs-regulations | 2026-10-21 | 2027-01-19 | 2 | --tax ownership --visa`

Precedent: the same prohibition was announced as an NCPRA amendment in the Annex to Budget Speech 2025-26 item 50(b) and Finance Act 2025 did not enact it (s.44 amends only s.3(3)(c)(i)). Para 182's levy WAS enacted, in narrower form: Finance Act 2026 s.9 inserts LDTA s.4(11), a 10% transferor-paid additional duty on transfers to a non-citizen of residential property on State land / Pas Géométriques (see the 2026-07-01 entry). ### 🇨🇳 CN

- `2026-10-01 | finance | First-home mortgage interest subsidy — 财金〔2026〕95号 (MoF + PBOC + NFRA, issued 29 Sep 2026), in force 1 Oct 2026 for a provisional one-year window. Covers commercial personal housing loans that banks newly disburse in the window to a resident household (居民家庭) buying its first commodity home (商品住房), new or second-hand; first home follows the current local first-home rules; home ≤120 m² gross floor area (建筑面积, incl. 公摊) and price ≤¥1.5M; refinancing of existing loans excluded. The state pays 1 percentage point a year on loan principal for up to 5 years, on at most ¥1M of principal per household, cost split 90% central / 10% local, no overall funding cap; the borrower authorises the bank when signing the loan contract and the bank deducts the subsidy from each monthly instalment (免申即享). Not stackable with a provident-fund loan or a loan-financed affordable-housing purchase (joint Q&A). Computed ceiling: ¥1M × 1 pp × 5 yrs = ¥50,000 of interest, less as principal amortises. Not addressed in the national texts: the commercial portion of a combination (组合) loan (a Guangxi Finance Department leaflet says it qualifies, seen only via an aggregator) and eligibility of foreign individuals — verify both with the lending bank. LPR context: 1-yr 3.0% and 5-yr-plus 3.5% since 20 May 2025, unchanged at the 20 Oct 2026 fixing. Check again before 30 Sep 2027 for any extension of the window | MoF notice https://m.mof.gov.cn/zcfb/202609/t20260929_3998312.htm + MoF/PBOC/NFRA Q&A https://www.pbc.gov.cn/goutongjiaoliu/113456/113469/2026092917153548424/index.html + PBOC LPR announcement 20 Oct 2026 | 2026-10-21 | 2027-04-19 | 3 | --finance (CN)`
- `2026-09-20 | finance | Housing Provident Fund Regulation (住房公积金管理条例) third revision — State Council Decree No. 844 (国务院关于修改《住房公积金管理条例》的决定), adopted at the 93rd State Council executive meeting 31 Jul 2026, signed by Premier Li Qiang 10 Aug 2026, reported by Xinhua 18 Aug 2026, in force 20 Sep 2026. Withdrawal grounds now nine (art. 24): rent withdrawals no longer need rent to exceed a share of household wage income; new grounds are renovation of one's own home, property-management fees for one's own home and other State Council-approved housing uses, with amounts set city by city. Withdrawals no longer need employer verification or a certificate, 3-day decision deadline unchanged (art. 25); loan decision deadline cut from 15 to 10 days (art. 26). Deposit and loan rates now decided by the State Council (art. 6; previously proposed by the PBoC after consulting MOHURD and approved by the State Council). Contribution floor stays 5% for employee and employer, now capped at the national maximum ratio (art. 18). Individual businesses, part-time and other flexible workers may contribute voluntarily under rules set by prefecture-level or higher governments (new art. 49). Fraud (new art. 41): fraudulently withdrawn funds returned plus a 3-year ban on withdrawals and HPF loans; a fraudulently obtained loan repaid plus a 5-year ban. Contribution records mutually recognised nationwide. Foreign employees' HPF participation remains a city-level matter — data not publicly available at national level, verify with the city HPF centre | Decree No. 844 text (gov.cn repost) https://www.mee.gov.cn/zcwj/gwywj/202608/t20260820_1164286.shtml + Xinhua via https://www.moj.gov.cn/pub/sfbgw/gwxw/xwyw/202608/t20260818_538627.html | 2026-10-21 | 2027-10-21 | 4 | --finance --work (CN)`
- `2026-08-28 | risks | New-build sales regime reform — 建房规〔2026〕3号 (MOHURD + MNR + NFRA, 《关于完善商品住房销售制度的通知》), issued and in force 28 Aug 2026, departmental normative document 现行有效. Presale: each building's main structure must be topped out first. Escrow: all buyer purchase funds (down payment and mortgage proceeds) go into a supervised account until completion acceptance and utilities are ready. Completed-home (现房) sales: projects on newly granted land, and granted-land projects with no planning permit, should prefer 现房 sales filed with the city or county housing department; projects with a planning permit are only encouraged to. Transition: projects that held a planning permit by 28 Aug 2026 keep the old presale and escrow rules. One lead bank per project; title at handover (交房即交证) is to be promoted, not a hard rule. Tier-1 implementing rules: Beijing 京建发〔2026〕460号 (24 Sep 2026; 现房 deposit generally ≤1% of price); Shanghai 沪建房管联〔2026〕451号 (in force 28 Sep 2026; generally ≤3%); Guangzhou 穗建房产【2026】599号 (30 Sep 2026; ≤5%, held in a supervised deposit account); Shenzhen 深建文〔2026〕131号 (15 Oct 2026; generally ≤3%). In all four, individual mortgages (incl. provident-fund loans) on projects that obtained a presale permit after 28 Aug 2026 are paid out only after completion filing, into the supervised account. Projects with land but no planning permit by 28 Aug 2026 may, with district-government sign-off and a filing, still presell under the old conditions until end-2027 (Shenzhen: 31 Dec 2027). Beijing and Shanghai say separate escrow-supervision measures will follow | MOHURD text via https://policy.mofcom.gov.cn/claw/clawContent.shtml?id=106751 + full text https://zjt.fujian.gov.cn/xxgk/gzdt/bmdt/202608/t20260828_7206278.htm + Beijing https://www.beijing.gov.cn/zhengce/zhengcefagui/202609/t20260924_4880146.html + Shanghai https://www.shanghai.gov.cn/gwk/search/content/aae26572365b49129bc4642a76a5040e + Guangzhou https://zfcj.gz.gov.cn/gkmlpt/content/11/11026/post_11026990.html + Shenzhen https://zjj.sz.gov.cn/xxgk/tzgg/content/post_13021746.html | 2026-10-21 | 2027-04-19 | 3 | --risks --type=off-plan (CN)`

- `2024-12-01 | tax | Deed-tax (契税) restructure eff. 1 Dec 2024 — MoF + STA + MOHURD Announcement 2024 No.16 (issued 12 Nov 2024), repealing Cai Shui [2016] No.23. Family-sole home ≤140m² 1% and >140m² 1.5%; family-SECOND home ≤140m² 1% and >140m² 2% — uniform nationwide. Exactly 140m² sits in the 1% band (140平方米及以下). CORRECTED: the announcement has no city carve-out but does NOT expressly name Beijing/Shanghai/Guangzhou/Shenzhen for deed tax — its only clause naming the four cities (Part 2(2)) is a VAT rule (once a city cancels the ordinary/non-ordinary housing standard, an individual's sale of a home held 2yr+ is VAT-exempt). - `2022-01-01 | tax | VAT standard rate cut 12%→10% effective 1 Jan 2022 (DIR VAT Guidance on 2021 Amendments) — a reduction, not an increase; 10% standard rate still in force as at the FY2026/27 Budget Communication (27 May 2026) and after the VAT (Amendment) (No. 2) Act 2026 (No. 19 of 2026, in force 1 Jul 2026), which does not touch the rate (its property change is the Bahamian-citizen first-home 4% band ceiling in Third Schedule item (3), $500,000 → $600,000 — no effect on foreign buyers); applies to property-related supplies incl. commercial rent (lease of commercial property at 10% regardless of value) and explicit HOA/management fees (DIR VAT FAQ Q13, Q17); unprepared/uncooked grocery food made VAT-exempt from 1 Apr 2026 (VAT (Amendment) Act 2026, No. 4 of 2026) does not affect property costs | DIR Department of Inland Revenue + https://inlandrevenue.finance.gov.bs/wp-content/uploads/2021/12/VAT-Guidance-2021-Amendments.pdf + DIR VAT FAQ https://inlandrevenue.finance.gov.bs/value-added-tax/faqs/ | 2026-10-21 | 2027-01-19 | 2 | --tax`
- `2026-07-01 in force (Act No. 21 of 2026); prior amendment 2025-07-01 (Act No. 43 of 2025) | tax | ENACTED: the Real Property Tax (Amendment) Act 2026 (No. 21 of 2026, assent 30 Jun 2026, Extraordinary Official Gazette 1 Jul 2026, s.1(2) "This Act shall come into force on the 1st day of July, 2026") enacted the former Bill 2026-0028 and amends the Real Property Tax Act Ch. 375 as previously amended by Act No. 43 of 2025. (1) New "foreign owner-occupied property" class: property owned by a non-Bahamian who holds a residence permit or annual work permit under the Immigration Act (Ch. 191) or a Home Owner Resident Permit under the International Persons Landholding Act (Ch. 140) and "occupies and resides in such property exclusively as a dwelling house on a permanent or seasonal basis" — taxed at "five-eighths of one per centum" (0.625%) on the part of the value up to $1,000,000 and 1% on the part above $1,000,000, with the maximum annual tax not to exceed $200,000 (no exempt first slice in the Act text). (2) "owner-occupied property" is restricted to a "Bahamian or the holder of certificate of permanent residence with a right to engage in gainful occupation", and the words "for a period of not less than 90 days in the year" are deleted. (3) The owner-occupied cap proviso is replaced by "the maximum annual tax rate shall not exceed one hundred and fifty thousand dollars" — the 183-day condition that the 2025 Act attached to the cap is gone. (4) s.42A(1) first-time-homeowner exemption now reads "Bahamian" person; s.42A(6) figure $500,000.00 → $600,000.00 — for a foreign buyer the Bahamian-only restriction is the operative change. (5) New s.3A: jointly owned property whose owners fall in different categories is apportioned equally and each share is taxed under its own owner's category. (6) New ss.25A–25Y power of sale: the Treasurer may sell property whose tax has been in arrears for more than twelve months, and these sections "shall not apply to owner-occupied property beneficially owned by a Bahamian" — foreign-owned property is within scope; new s.25Z also lets the Treasurer acquire property other than Bahamian-owned owner-occupied property where tax plus penalties and interest exceed the assessed value, with the assessed value applied to the arrears. (7) New s.3(2)(bb) proviso: in a duplex, triplex or fourplex the unit classified owner-occupied under s.3(8B) is taxed at the owner-occupied rate and the aggregate annual tax on the remaining unit or units may not exceed $300,000. The FY2026/27 Budget Communication's "former 180-day test" wording never had a statutory counterpart (the 2025 Act said 90 and 183) — do not carry 180 days. The official tabled-bills page still lists "Real Property Tax (Amendment) Bill, 2026" (a list of bills tabled in 2026, not evidence of non-enactment). WARNING: DIR RPT FAQ Q10 (page last modified 28 Apr 2023, unchanged on 2026-10-21) still lists only owner-occupied (1st $300,000 exempt / 0.625% next $200,000 / 1% above $500,000), residential, commercial-or-foreign-owned-rental and vacant-land-for-foreigners bands, with no foreign owner-occupied category — DIR guidance is behind the 2026 Act, so confirm classification and bands with DIR in writing before any carry-cost or transaction-cost computation | Real Property Tax (Amendment) Act 2026 No. 21 of 2026 https://laws.bahamas.gov.bs/cms/images/LEGISLATION/AMENDING/2026/2026-0021A/2026-0021A.pdf + Real Property Tax (Amendment) Act 2025 No. 43 of 2025 https://laws.bahamas.gov.bs/cms/images/LEGISLATION/AMENDING/2025/2025-0043A/2025-0043A.pdf + consolidated index https://laws.bahamas.gov.bs/cms/legislation/index/by-alphabetical.html + tabled bills https://laws.bahamas.gov.bs/cms/legislation/tabled-in-parliament/tabled-in-house-of-assembly.html + FY2026-27 Budget Communication https://bahamasbudget.gov.bs + DIR RPT FAQ https://inlandrevenue.finance.gov.bs/real-property-tax/faqs-rpt/ | 2026-10-21 | 2027-01-19 | 1 | --tax --home-tax`

- `2026-10-19 (signed; not in force) | other | San Marino–EU Association Agreement (same agreement as the AD entry; official title 'Agreement establishing an association between the European Union and its Member States, of the one part, and the Principality of Andorra and the Republic of San Marino, each as a separate Party, of the other part', so a mixed agreement) — SIGNED 19 Oct 2026 in Brussels by the Commission, San Marino and Andorra (Commission IP/26/2182; EEAS; Govern d'Andorra AA portal); the amended EU–San Marino Monetary Agreement was signed the same day. The Council authorised signature and provisional application in July 2026 (Council press release dated 16 Jul 2026). Next steps per the Commission: the Council requests European Parliament consent before concluding the Agreement (EP Legislative Observatory 2024/0101(NLE), checked 2026-10-28: 'Preparatory phase in Parliament', indicative plenary sitting date 18 Jan 2027, a forecast), and the Agreement 'will be ready for provisional application as soon as the respective ratification procedures in Andorra and San Marino allow'; as a mixed agreement it also needs ratification by each member state (Govern d'Andorra explainer); per the Govern d'Andorra the signature brings neither entry into force nor provisional application. Provisional-application terms per the Council decision text (Council doc ST 5290/25 of 4 Mar 2026, linked from the esteri.sm Accordo page; the adopted decision is not yet in the Official Journal per the EU Publications Office index searched 2026-10-28): Art 8 — parts of the Agreement apply provisionally under Agreement Art 112(2) from the first day of the month following the month in which a Contracting Party deposits its instrument of ratification, conclusion or approval with the Council Secretary-General, unless another Contracting Party notifies that provisional application cannot take place, and the start date is published in the Official Journal; Art 9 — everything applies provisionally except Arts 27–32 (to the extent they concern indirect investments) and Art 63. The esteri.sm Accordo page itself did not record the 19 Oct 2026 signature when checked 2026-10-28 (its list of national acts ends at the Consiglio Grande e Generale decision of 14 Jul 2025). News-only (San Marino RTV, Oct 2026): the Secretary of State for Foreign Affairs expects to put the Agreement to the Consiglio Grande e Generale for ratification around November 2026, with notification of provisional application possibly in early 2027. News-only (Libertas and GiornaleSM/askanews, 21 Oct 2026): on 21 Oct 2026 the Secretary of State for Internal Affairs told the Consiglio Grande e Generale the text will be put to it for ratification at the first useful sitting. Primary (CGG convocation of 16 Oct 2026): the session of 21 Oct–6 Nov 2026 has no item on the Association Agreement among its 33 agenda items. Secondary (strategic.sm, 4 Oct 2026, LOW): Commissione Esteri and Consiglio Grande e Generale examination Oct–Dec 2026, provisional application from the first day of the month after San Marino notifies ratification with some parts including tax cooperation excluded, 1 Jan 2027 the target if the steps proceed smoothly; a post-signature referendum has been raised in political debate, not decided; the Govern d'Andorra release of 19 Oct 2026 says the Agreement enters into force in San Marino without any later public consultation, so the two sources differ on a referendum step. Not in force and not provisionally applied as at 2026-10-28 (Commission IP/26/2182 re-checked 2026-10-28; computed from Art 8 above and that agenda, not stated by any source: with no ratification item in the session running to 6 Nov 2026, no San Marino deposit is in prospect before November, so the earliest possible provisional-application start is now 1 Dec 2026). - `pending (signing proposal COM(2026) 442 of 7 Sep 2026; not signed as of 2026-10-28) | visa | EU–San Marino 'Agreement on several aspects in the field of border management' — NOT Schengen association (Council negotiating mandate Decision (EU) 2024/1733 of 30 May 2024, OJ L 20.6.2024): negotiations concluded 26 Jan 2026, initialled 21 Apr 2026; on 7 Sep 2026 the Commission proposed signature and provisional application (COM(2026) 442, 2026/0245(NLE)) and conclusion after EP consent (COM(2026) 443, 2026/0246(NLE)). Annexed text: no border checks between San Marino and the Schengen States (art. 11); San Marino residence permits give 90/180-day visa-free Schengen access with EES and ETIAS exemption (arts. 21–22); a third-country national's permit is issued or renewed only after an Italian security check (art. 25) and only on a 'genuine connection' — actual and regular physical presence; 'predetermined investments having been made in the Republic of San Marino's economy or real estate' do not count (art. 24(2)); permits issued before it applies stay valid at most two years (art. 26(1)); police cooperation incl. cross-border surveillance and hot pursuit (arts. 32–33); San Marino takes on no EU visa policy (Annex I lists only Reg (EC) 1030/2002, the permit format; art. 46(2) review if it starts issuing visas). 'Third-country national' excludes EU, Icelandic, Liechtenstein, Norwegian, Swiss, Andorran and Monegasque citizens (art. 9(16)). Provisional application from the 1st day of the month after both sides notify (art. 55(2)). As of 2026-10-28: no Council signing decision in the OJ (Publications Office SPARQL, index current to 28 Oct 2026); EP file at 'Preparatory phase in Parliament', only event 07/09/2026 (OEIL). Reaches the Residenza Elettiva €500k property route once applied | COM(2026) 442 + annex via EU Publications Office Cellar `https://publications.europa.eu/resource/celex/52026PC0442` + OJ `https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/dec/2024/1733/oj` + EP Legislative Observatory `https://oeil.europarl.europa.eu/oeil/en/procedure-file?reference=2026/0246(NLE)` | 2026-10-28 | 2027-01-26 | 3 | --visa --retirement --digital-nomad`

- `2025-01-01 | tax | Stamp Duties (Amendment) Act 2024 (Act No. 33 of 2024, assented 9 Dec 2024, gazetted 14 Dec 2024) in force 1 January 2025 — new s.64(12) charges duty on any conveyance or transfer of stock or shares by sale, exchange or gift, including joint tenancy conveyances or transfers for the portion of interest transferred to a party other than the transferor, at 5% for shares in companies incorporated in Belize and 7% for shares in companies incorporated outside Belize, on value or consideration whichever is greater, so the share-transfer route into a property-holding company is taxed rather than exempt (the Act does not mention IBCs); s.59 re-bands mortgage duty to 0% up to BZD 100,000 secured, 1.5% on the excess above BZD 100,000 for mortgages and 0.6% on the excess for assignment or transfer of mortgage and for reconveyance, release, cancellation, discharge or surrender; s.69 replaced with a new exemption list including conveyances between spouses, common-law spouses, siblings, children and parents, testamentary disposition and intestacy, and agricultural or residential leases under 2 years at rent under BZD 2,000 per month (no grandparent or grandchild exemption despite agent marketing claiming one). CORRECTED 2026-10-21: s.69(d) is not a BZD 25,000 cap on charitable transfers — it exempts a voluntary disposition "if such conveyance is in favour of any charitable organisation or of the trustees of a trust established for charitable purposes only or where the total value of all the properties so conveyed by the transferor in the twelve months ending on the date of the conveyance concerned is not more than twenty-five thousand dollars", so the statutory wording sets no cap on gifts to a charity or charitable trust and makes the BZD 25,000-in-12-months test a separate alternative for voluntary dispositions (confirm how the Commissioner of Stamps applies it with a Belize attorney); s.72 extends land-transfer duty to joint tenancy conveyances and exempts company-to-company transfers where the beneficial owner as defined in s.2(1) Money Laundering and Terrorism (Prevention) Act is the same on both sides. s.69(g) later amended by Act No. 7 of 2025 (gazetted 11 Feb 2025, also in force 1 January 2025 — on 2026-10-21 seen only in a search-engine index of the Assembly PDF, which could not be opened). ⚠️ A social-media search snippet attributed to the National Assembly's 12 Aug 2026 Finance and Economic Development Committee meeting lists 'A new bill to streamline property transaction taxes, replacing outdated sections of the Stamp Duties Act' (snippet only — the post itself not read); no such Bill or Act was found in the sources read on 2026-10-21, so check for a newer Act. - `2025-10-30 | foreign-buyer | CCJ ENDED its decade-long monitoring of the 2015 Consent Order at the Final Monitoring Hearing on 30 Oct 2025. CCJ President Winston Anderson: "That supervision ends today", citing the January 2022 Maya of Southern Belize Free Prior and Informed Consultation Protocol and "the adoption in September 2025 of a working draft on Recognition and Protection of Maya Customary Land Tenure in Southern Belize, along with the formulation of drafting instructions and the selection/engagement of a draftsperson". The Court closed its MONITORING role only, not its jurisdiction — "The Court will, of course, remain of service to both sides in its regular capacity". Subject-specific legislation is still NOT enacted: the Court said "The ultimate goal was to have subject-specific legislation enacted" and that its hope is that implementation of the 2015 Order "will proceed through its legislative and other phases". On 30 Jul 2026 the CCJ dismissed the Maya Leaders Alliance / Toledo Alcaldes Association application filed 14 May 2026 in BZCV2014/002 seeking clarification of the 2015 Consent Order. Its written reasons ([2026] CCJ 13 (AJ) BZ, dated 2 Oct 2026) name the draft as the Maya Customary Land Tenure (Recognition and Protection of Rights) Bill, 2025, record that the Working Draft went to a legal draftsperson on 11 Sep 2025 and the Draft Bill to a Review Panel of the parties' representatives on 21 Oct 2025, state "The Draft Bill has yet to be laid before, debated or enacted by the National Assembly", conclude that the Court "ought not to intervene at this point in the way asked for by the Appellants", and say that whether the Draft Bill, if enacted, is consistent with ss 3, 3(d), 16 and 17 of the Constitution is justiciable by way of constitutional challenge. At the 30 Jul hearing Justice Anderson said "The precise scope and extent of Maya Customary Land Tenure will therefore necessarily will remain inchoate and uncertain until authoritatively identified and codified by the laws of Belize" (⚠️ 7News Belize 30 Jul 2026 — secondary; the phrase is not in the written reasons). Minister of Indigenous Peoples Affairs Dr Louis Zabaneh, at a National Consultation and Information-Sharing Session in Punta Gorda Town, said the draft "now goes through a review panel where we have membership from the government on the panel, from the MLA /TAA and from private land owners" before returning to Cabinet and only then going to the National Assembly (⚠️ Love FM 5 Aug 2026 — secondary). After the reasons were released, Mr E Andrew Marshalleck Jr SC, counsel for the Attorney General, said the review panel would be scheduled "as quickly as we can get the group together", and the Maya Leaders Alliance said it had proposed changes to the draft, with another review panel meeting expected in early November 2026 (⚠️ Love FM and Greater Belize Media 6 Oct 2026 — secondary). The National Assembly Draft Bills 2026 register listed no Maya, indigenous or customary-land-tenure Bill when last read on 2026-08-07 (16 bills listed); the nationalassembly.gov.bz host returned 'This Account has been suspended' on 2026-10-21. Buyer position unchanged: Maya customary land tenure is "a constitutionally protected right" per Maya Leaders Alliance v Attorney General of Belize [2015] CCJ 15 (AJ), but communal title for the Maya villages of the Toledo District is still not fixed in statute (2026 data) — the boundary between registered private title and customary tenure remains unlegislated, so confirm current Bill/Act status against the National Assembly and a Belize attorney before any Toledo-District deal | CCJ President's Closing Remarks at the Final Monitoring Hearing 30 Oct 2025 — `https://ccj.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/Anderson_20251030.pdf` + CCJ Reasons for Decision [2026] CCJ 13 (AJ) BZ, 2 Oct 2026 — `https://ccj.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/2026_CCJ_13_AJ_BZ.pdf` + National Assembly of Belize Draft Bills 2026 register — `https://www.nationalassembly.gov.bz/draft-bills-2026` + (⚠️ media) 7News Belize 30 Jul 2026 — `https://www.7newsbelize.com/printstory.php?func=print&nid=79319` + (⚠️ media) Love FM Belize 5 Aug 2026 — `https://lovefm.com/government-considers-expanding-maya-land-rights-consultations-nationwide` + (⚠️ media) Love FM Belize 6 Oct 2026 — `https://lovefm.com/ccj-declines-to-intervene-in-maya-customary-land-rights-legislation` + (⚠️ media) Greater Belize Media 6 Oct 2026 — `https://www.greaterbelize.com/ccj-sends-maya-land-fight-back-to-belizean-lawmakers` | 2026-10-21 | 2027-01-19 | 1 | --visa ownership --notary`

- `2001-08-30 | foreign-buyer | ENFORCEMENT-TREND CLAIM WITHDRAWN and replaced with the statutory position. Two verification passes (2026-08-07) located no MLMUPC or GDCG prakas, circular or statement evidencing a 2024-2026 anti-nominee campaign, and the US State Department 2025 Investment Climate Statement for Cambodia does not use the word nominee anywhere in the report as fetched 2026-08-07 (0 occurrences again on 2026-10-21; the 2026 ICS, PDF dated September 2026, also has none) — so "under enforcement pressure" and "increasingly challenged" were unsourced and are removed. The statute is the stronger claim and is primary-quotable. Constitution Art. 44: "Only natural persons or legal entities of Khmer nationality shall have the right to own land." Land Law 2001 (Royal Kram NS/RKM/0801/14, promulgated 30 August 2001) Art. 9 expressly PERMITS a company with 51 percent or more Cambodian-held shares to own land, but adds: "Only percentages stipulated in the articles of incorporation are taken into account. Any private agreement signed by a shareholder that is contrary to this article is null and void." The side-agreement, share-pledge and power-of-attorney bundle marketed as making a nominee land-holding company safe for the foreign funder is therefore VOID BY STATUTE from the day it is signed, not merely exposed to enforcement risk — the structure is legal, the foreign buyer's protection inside it is not. Land Law Art. 8: a foreigner who "falsifies national identity to become an owner of land in Cambodia shall be punished as determined under article 251" (Art. 251: "imprisonment from one (1) to five (5) years") and "Any property bought under these circumstances will be seized as state property without compensation from the state." CITE Land Law Art. 9, NOT Sub-Decree 111 Art. 3.2 — Sub-Decree 111 (2005) is a QIP-incentives instrument and its successor Sub-Decree 139 of 26 June 2023 carries no Use of Nominees clause (0 occurrences of nominee, re-checked 2026-10-21). The Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code (2011) Art. 80, which omits or rewrites many Land Law articles, leaves Arts. 8, 9 and 251 untouched. WATCH (corrected 2026-10-21): the 2026 ICS says "Cambodia is drafting a new land law which would include articles" requiring landowners to be Cambodian citizens, clarifying tax payment obligations, detailing co-ownership rights and promoting a digital cadastral system (the 2025 ICS said it was "still undergoing stakeholder consultation"); draft text HAS circulated — Open Development Cambodia (NGO data hub, secondary) hosts three drafts listing MLMUPC as issuer (2023 1st draft, 26 March 2024 2nd draft, 19 June 2024 3rd draft), contents not reviewed here; no Council of Ministers or National Assembly adoption located — no forecast made | Constitution of the Kingdom of Cambodia Art. 44 via Ministry of Foreign Affairs and International Cooperation `https://mfaic.gov.kh/publish/files/uploads/2Z87AYOO63VI/Constitution%20of%20the%20Kingdom%20of%20Cambodia.pdf` + Land Law 2001 Arts. 8, 9 and 251 via `https://asset.cambodia.gov.kh/mowa/2016/12/land-law-2001-kh-eng1.pdf` (unofficial English translation, Khmer version official) + Council for the Development of Cambodia `https://cdc.gov.kh/laws-and-regulations/land` + Sub-Decree 139 of 26 June 2023 `https://cib-cdc.gov.kh/media/2025/04/2.-Sub-Decree-139-on-Implementing-LOI-2023-EN.pdf` + US State Department 2025 Investment Climate Statement Cambodia `https://www.state.gov/reports/2025-investment-climate-statements/cambodia` + US State Department 2026 Investment Climate Statement Cambodia `https://www.state.gov/reports/2026-investment-climate-statements/cambodia` + Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code via CDC `https://cdc.gov.kh/wp-content/uploads/2022/04/Law-on-Implementation-of-CC_Eng_FINAL_110531.pdf` + Open Development Cambodia draft Land Law record (secondary) `https://data.opendevelopmentcambodia.net/laws_record/draft-land-law` | 2026-10-21 | 2027-01-19 | 1 | --visa ownership --integrity`
- `2019-01-02 (Royal Kram; constitutionally untested) | foreign-buyer | Law on Trust (Royal Kram NS/RKM/0119/002, made 02 Jan 2019; adopted by the National Assembly 13 Dec 2018; declared urgent by Art. 57) over land — whether a foreign-beneficiary trust over land survives the Constitution Art. 44 bar on foreign land ownership is untested: no Constitutional Council decision located (2026-10-21). The Law's own Art. 16 caps an individual-interest trust at 100 years and provides that if, on termination, the beneficiary "has not received the permission or the rights in possession of the property which is the land, then the trust property must become the tenancy until the beneficiary has the full right in possessing the land in accordance with the applicable law" — the statute defers to land-ownership law at termination rather than overriding it. The unofficial English translation contains no reference to foreigners or nationality (glossary: Beneficiary = "a person who benefits from the Trust"). US State Department 2026 ICS: the Trust Law allows "investors to own or transfer landed property via a regulated trust mechanism", the market is "still developing and subject to regulatory updates", and 1,712 trusts worth about $2.5 billion were registered in 2025. The Trust Regulator site lists 2026 prakas and guidelines (dispute resolution, branches, AML sanctions, ethics, trustee operations, customer due diligence) — none located on land or foreign beneficiaries. Do NOT rely on a trust as a route to foreign land ownership until tested | Law on Trust (unofficial English translation) via Trust Regulator `https://www.trustregulator.gov.kh/storage/lawregulations/January2024/Ko8e0it5UbPMSXND7No2.pdf` + Trust Regulator `https://www.trustregulator.gov.kh/` + US State Department 2026 Investment Climate Statement Cambodia `https://www.state.gov/reports/2026-investment-climate-statements/cambodia` | 2026-10-21 | 2027-01-19 | 2 | --visa ownership --integrity`

The Fund's position is that hosts already owe the levy (its chair, Jan 2026: no legal gap); its levy page applies the 2 % to all regulated hotels, restaurants and any regulated tourism activities, and furnished apartments and villas are listed in the 4th Schedule of the Tourism Regulations 2014 — model 2 % of gross as a possible liability for TRA-licensable STR; platform collection at source pending, no date. - `2024-XX-XX (Gazette commencement not primary-confirmed) | ownership | Public-land lease extension raised from 75 to 99 years — Bill for the Land Leases (Amendment) Act No. of 2024 (Parliament's Second Ordinary Session 2024; commences on Gazette publication) amends Land Leases Act [Cap 163] s.32B(1): delete "75 years" (second occurring), substitute "99 years". The Minister MAY therefore extend a lease of PUBLIC land (s.32A: land declared public under the Land Reform Act [Cap 123] or any other Act, or acquired under the Land Acquisition Act [Cap 215]) whose term is under 75 years to 99 years, on the lessee's application, for a premium set by the Principal Valuation Officer of no more than 10 percent of unimproved market value at the application date, and only if the lessee has complied with all lease conditions (s.32B(2)-(4)); a public-land lease with a 75-year term is instead renewable for up to 75 years under s.32C (apply no later than 3 months before expiry; discretionary). Custom-land leases have no statutory extension or renewal path. s.32(1)-(2) (no grant or extension beyond 75 years) is not amended by the bill, and how it interacts with the amended s.32B is unresolved in the text. Status: passed by Parliament per the Minister of Lands, who announced implementation on 7 Feb 2026 with more than 2,000 leases eligible (Vanuatu Daily Post, news-only); the Ministry has since registered the first 99-year state-land lease extension in Port Vila, reported cost ~VT7 million per application, more than 200 applications lodged (Vanuatu Daily Post, undated online article, news-only). Enacted Act number, assent and Gazette commencement NOT primary-confirmed — verify at PacLII or the Official Gazette. The FAOLEX Cap 163 text (Consolidated Edition 2006; ss.32A-32C inserted by Act No. 24 of 2003) predates the amendment | Parliament of Vanuatu bill https://parliament.gov.vu/images/Bills%20for%202024%20Second%20ordinary/Bill%20for%20land%20leases/Bill%20for%20the%20Land%20Leases%20Am%20Act%20No.%20%20of%202024.pdf + Land Leases Act [Cap 163] Consolidated Edition 2006 https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/van88905.pdf + Vanuatu Daily Post 7 Feb 2026 https://www.dailypost.vu/news/lands-ministry-to-roll-out-99-year-lease-amendment-next-week/article_edaa0e26-b0d6-565d-8f8b-94d95adea7f5.html + Vanuatu Daily Post first 99-year lease https://www.dailypost.vu/news/vanuatu-registers-first-ever-99-year-state-land-lease/article_c43674b1-f64b-593b-af44-a262a760e586.html | 2026-10-23 | 2027-01-21 | 2 | --price --risks --exit --finance --notary (VU)`
- `2025-02-03 | visa | EU PERMANENTLY revoked Vanuatu's visa-free Schengen access — Regulation (EU) 2025/11 of 19 Dec 2024 (Council decision 12 Dec 2024; OJ L 14 Jan 2025; enters into force on the twentieth day after publication) moved Vanuatu from Annex II Part 1 to Annex I Part 1 of Reg (EU) 2018/1806, and the permanent visa requirement APPLIES FROM 3 Feb 2025 (Danish Immigration Service, page updated 2025-02-03). Chain: partial suspension from 4 May 2022 for ordinary passports issued as of 25 May 2015 (Council Decision (EU) 2022/366 of 3 Mar 2022) → full suspension of the visa-waiver Agreement by Council Decision (EU) 2022/2198 of 8 Nov 2022 → visa required 4 Feb 2023 (Commission Delegated Reg (EU) 2023/222 of 1 Dec 2022, suspension running to 3 Feb 2025) → permanent from 3 Feb 2025. The first use of the suspension clause in the EU visa-exemption rules (European Parliament press release 21 Nov 2024), and STILL IN FORCE — EUR-Lex lists Reg (EU) 2025/11 as in force and DG HOME's visa-policy page states the visa waiver agreement with Vanuatu has been suspended under the suspension mechanism; the same page notes the mechanism was revised by Reg (EU) 2025/2441 of 26 Nov 2025 (OJ 10 Dec 2025), whose grounds include operation of an investor citizenship scheme. CBI CORRECTED: the DSP and CIIP donation routes are not property-linked (the receiving fund is named the National Development Fund by secondary sources only), BUT the Vanuatu Citizenship Office lists a property-linked Real Estate Option Program (REO) among its current citizenship types — commenced 23 Jul 2021 under s.13F Citizenship Act [Cap 112] and the Citizenship (Real Estate Option Program) Regulation Order No. 93 of 2021; the applicant must purchase a property in a Vanuatu Government Approved Project (four listed: Pacific Springs, Narpow Point Coral Bay and FPF Rainbow City Project on Efate; Milai on Espiritu Santo); no suspension or closure notice on the Office's pages; REO minimum price not published by the Office — data not publicly available, verify at the Vanuatu Citizenship Office. OECD high-risk-list status not re-checked this run (list not machine-readable) — verify at oecd.org | EUR-Lex CELEX 32025R0011 + Council press release 2024-12-12 + https://home-affairs.ec.europa.eu/policies/schengen/visa-policy_en + https://vancitizenship.gov.vu/index.php/news-and-updates/90-commencement-of-the-reo-program + https://www.nyidanmark.dk/en-GB/Words-and-concepts/US/Visum/Visa---Reintroduction-of-the-visa-requirement-for-citizens-of-Vanuatu | 2026-10-21 | 2027-01-19 | 2 | --visa (VU)`

### 🇹🇹 TT

- `2026-01-01 (REPEALED — Finance Act 2025 s.17, in force under s.23(1)) | tax | Property Tax Act (Chap. 76:04) REPEALED with effect from 1 Jan 2026 — no annual property tax for 2026 onward. CORRECTED: the earlier "halted 2025, Act and 2% rate still on the books" reading is wrong. Finance Act, 2025 (Act No. 14 of 2025; passed House 5 Dec, Senate 9 Dec, assented 23 Dec 2025; Legal Supplement Part A to the Gazette Vol. 64 No. 188, 25 Dec 2025) s.17: "The Property Tax Act is repealed."; s.23(1): sections "... 14, 15, 16, 17, 18, ... come into force on 1st January, 2026"; s.13 deletes "or the Property Tax Act" from Valuation of Land Act s.27(1). Minister of Finance, House 5 Dec 2025 (unrevised Hansard): "clause 17 repeals the hated Property Tax Act, Chap. 76:04". NOTHING REPLACES IT: Act No. 14 of 2025 creates no land or building tax, the Finance Act 2026 (Act No. 16 of 2026) has no property-tax provision, and the FY2027 Budget Statement (12 Oct 2026, 204 pp.) does not mention property tax; the 2009 Act had itself repealed the Lands and Buildings Taxes Act and Part V (rates) of the Municipal Corporations Act (PTA ss.55-56), and a repeal does not revive a law not in force (Interpretation Act Chap. 3:01 s.27(1)(a)). The Landlord Business Surcharge (MTA Part XVII, in force 1 Jan 2026, separate entry) taxes rental receipts, not ownership. History: reintroduced 2024 at residential 2% of Annual Taxable Value (ATV = ARV less a 10% voids deduction, ≈1.8% of ARV) after the Property Tax (Amendment) Act 2024 (Act No. 3 of 2024, assented 27 Mar 2024) cut 3% → 2%; IRD voided every Notice of Assessment dated before 6 Mar 2024 and reissued it at 2%, payment due 30 Sep 2024; collection halted in 2025 (FY2026 Budget Statement, 13 Oct 2025, p.22: "We halted the Property Tax that created anxiety for thousands of people, including the elderly, retired and low-income"). ARREARS / REFUNDS OPEN: Act No. 14 of 2025 has no savings, waiver or refund clause; Interpretation Act s.27(1)(c) keeps a liability already accrued under a repealed law unless a contrary intention appears, so 2024-25 assessments are not extinguished by the repeal itself (statutory default). Whether IRD collects or waives them, and whether 2024 payments are refunded, is data not publicly available (no IRD notice on its news list, newest items Sep 2026) — before closing, get IRD confirmation that no property-tax assessment is outstanding on the property | Finance Act 2025 `https://www.ttparliament.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/a2025-14g.pdf` (same text, Government Printer e-gazette `https://printery.gov.tt/e-gazette/2025/Acts/Act_No._14_of_2025.pdf`) + House Hansard 5 Dec 2025 `https://www.ttparliament.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/hh20251205u.pdf` + Property Tax Act ss.54-57 (laws.gov.tt) `http://laws.gov.tt/ttdll-web/revision/download/106664?type=act` + Interpretation Act s.27 (laws.gov.tt) `http://laws.gov.tt/ttdll-web/revision/download/92673?type=act` + FY2026 Budget Statement `https://www.finance.gov.tt/wp-content/uploads/2025/10/Budget-Statement-FY-2026-1.pdf` + FY2027 Budget Statement `https://www.finance.gov.tt/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/2027-Budget-Statement-14-10-26.pdf` + https://www.ird.gov.tt/news/amended-property-tax-notices-of-assessment | 2026-10-28 | 2027-01-19 | 1 | --tax --rental (TT)`
- `2026-06-25 (Finance Act 2026 assented — no commencement section) | tax | Landlord Business Surcharge (LBS) income-tax credit — the surcharge itself (2.5% of gross quarterly rent up to TT$20,000, 3.5% above; one-time TT$2,500 registration fee) is Part XVII (ss.79-91) of the Miscellaneous Taxes Act, inserted by the Finance Act, 2025 (Act No. 14 of 2025) and in force 1 Jan 2026; that Act carries no income-tax credit. The credit comes from the Finance Act, 2026 (Act No. 16 of 2026, assented 25 Jun 2026, Legal Supplement Part A Vol. 65 No. 111, 29 Jun 2026): s.21(d) inserts Income Tax Act s.48O (individual landlords) and s.22(b) inserts Corporation Tax Act s.16M (company landlords) — a credit against that year of income's tax liability up to a maximum of that liability, so surcharge above the landlord's annual tax liability is not credited (inference: for a landlord with little or no taxable income it is a real cost, not only a timing cost). The Act does not state the year of income from which the credit applies — whether LBS paid for Q1-Q2 2026 is creditable is data not publicly available, verify with the BIR. The same Act (s.23) amends the MTA: the one-time fee is due irrespective of the number of premises let (s.81(1)(b)); the public landlord register and certified extracts are repealed (s.81(4)-(6)); copies of the registration certificate are allowed (s.82); the surcharge is payable in each quarter ending 31 Mar, 30 Jun, 30 Sep and 31 Dec (s.83(1) substituted), and the per-payment return and TT$100 late-return penalty are repealed (s.83(2)-(3)); the Minister may amend the exempt list by Order (new s.85(1A)). Registration deadline 30 May 2026. FY2027 Budget Statement (delivered 12 Oct 2026): 18,678 landlords registered, TT$48.4m in registration fees, TT$85.6m in surcharge payments; no new LBS measure announced | Finance Act 2026 `https://www.ttparliament.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/a2026-16g.pdf` + Finance Bill 2026 explanatory note `https://www.ttparliament.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/06/b2026h12.pdf` + FY2027 Budget Statement `https://www.finance.gov.tt/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/2027-Budget-Statement-14-10-26.pdf` | 2026-10-23 | 2027-04-19 | 4 | --tax --rental (TT)`

- `2026-01-22 | ownership | Law on Non-Saudis Ownership of Real Estate (نظام تملك غير السعوديين للعقار), Royal Decree M/14 of 19/1/1447H (14 Jul 2025) on Council of Ministers Decision 42 of 13/1/1447H (8 Jul 2025); REGA lists its publication date as 30/1/1447H (25 Jul 2025); IN FORCE 22 Jan 2026 — REGA's own notice announces entry into force from Thursday 3 Sha'ban 1447H = 22 Jan 2026 (art. 15: 180 days after gazette publication), which settles the 21-vs-22 Jan split. Art. 14: it replaces the 2000 Law of Real Estate Ownership and Investment by Non-Saudis (RD M/15 of 17/4/1421H) and repeals conflicting provisions; the decree also repeals RD 44 of 29/11/1377H and preserves (a) real-estate rights lawfully acquired by non-Saudis before entry into force and (b) existing statutory bans on ownership in specified places; CoM Decision 42 item 4 orders the steps to cancel art. 4 of the GCC-citizens ownership regulation (RD M/22 of 3/4/1432H) as it relates to property inside Makkah and Madinah, effective from M/14's entry into force. REVERSES the prior assertion in `shared/notary-forced-heirship.md` that Mecca and Medina are closed to all non-Saudis: art. 2(4) limits the art. 2(1) right in the cities of Makkah and Madinah to Muslim natural persons (a non-Muslim may not take title); art. 2(3) lets a lawfully resident non-Saudi natural person own one residence outside the Cabinet-designated scope, Makkah and Madinah excepted (exec. reg. art. 7: spouse and non-Saudi descendants count as dependants until the marriage ends or the descendant turns 25). CORRECTED company limb (was 'unlisted foreign companies only for operational requirements or staff accommodation'): art. 3(1) lets an unlisted company incorporated under the Saudi Companies Law with non-Saudi shareholders own inside the designated scope INCLUDING Makkah and Madinah, with no MISA approval (exec. reg. art. 8(3)); outside the scope it may own only for its activities and staff housing, with MISA approval and EXCLUDING Makkah and Madinah (exec. reg. art. 8(1)-(2)); art. 4: listed companies, investment funds and licensed SPEs own anywhere including the two cities under Capital Market Authority rules; a non-Saudi company registers with MISA and discloses direct and indirect owners first (exec. reg. art. 3). IMPLEMENTATION: on 23 Jun 2026 (8/1/1448H) the Council of Ministers approved the executive regulation (Decision 43 of 8/1/1448H, Umm Al-Qura 18/1/1448H = 3 Jul 2026) and the geographic scopes in which non-Saudis may own (scope maps with ownership percentages, right types and usufruct periods on REGA's Saudi Properties portal). FEE: law art. 9 caps a REGA fee on a non-Saudi's disposal of real rights at 5% of value; exec. reg. art. 9 sets 2% for the city of Riyadh, the city of Makkah, the city of Madinah and Jeddah governorate (all right types, all uses) and art. 10 zero-rates disposals elsewhere plus estate partition (قسمة التركة), final court orders, expropriation, gratuitous transfers to a waqf or public body and other listed cases. Whether a non-Muslim heir may take title to Makkah / Madinah property is not addressed in the law or the regulation text — data not publicly available — verify at REGA / Ministry of Justice. Verified 2026-10-21 | Umm Al-Qura law text https://www.uqn.gov.sa/details?p=27293 + REGA law page with decree and CoM decision https://rega.gov.sa/%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A3%D9%86%D8%B8%D9%85%D8%A9-%D9%88%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%82%D8%B1%D8%A7%D8%B1%D8%A7%D8%AA/%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A3%D9%86%D8%B8%D9%85%D8%A9-%D9%88%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%84%D9%88%D8%A7%D8%A6%D8%AD-%D9%88%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A3%D8%AF%D9%84%D8%A9/%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A3%D9%86%D8%B8%D9%85%D8%A9/%D9%86%D8%B8%D8%A7%D9%85-%D8%AA%D9%85%D9%84%D9%83-%D8%BA%D9%8A%D8%B1-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B3%D8%B9%D9%88%D8%AF%D9%8A%D9%8A%D9%86-%D9%84%D9%84%D8%B9%D9%82%D8%A7%D8%B1/ + Umm Al-Qura executive regulation 3 Jul 2026 https://www.uqn.gov.sa/decisions-and-regulations/4001281 + Umm Al-Qura Council of Ministers session 23 Jun 2026 https://www.uqn.gov.sa/news/council-of-ministers/4001222 + REGA in-force notice 22 Jan 2026 https://rega.gov.sa/media-center/%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A3%D8%AE%D8%A8%D8%A7%D8%B1-%D9%88%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A5%D8%B9%D9%84%D8%A7%D9%86%D8%A7%D8%AA/%D9%87%D9%8A%D8%A6%D8%A9-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B9%D9%82%D8%A7%D8%B1-%D8%AA%D8%B9%D9%84%D9%86-%D8%A8%D8%AF%D8%A1-%D9%86%D9%81%D8%A7%D8%B0-%D9%86%D8%B8%D8%A7%D9%85-%D8%AA%D9%85%D9%84-%D9%83-%D8%BA%D9%8A%D8%B1-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B3%D8%B9%D9%88%D8%AF%D9%8A%D9%8A%D9%86-%D9%84%D9%84%D8%B9%D9%82%D8%A7%D8%B1-%D8%A7%D8%B9%D8%AA%D8%A8%D8%A7%D8%B1-%D8%A7-%D9%85%D9%86-%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%8A%D9%88%D9%85 + REGA programme page https://rega.gov.sa/rega-services/platforms/non-saudi-real-estate-ownership-in-saudi-arabia | 2026-10-21 | 2027-01-19 | 1 | --notary --price (SA)`
- `2025-08-22 | tax | SA White Land Tax superseded — the law was amended by Royal Decree M/244 of 7/11/1446H after Council of Ministers approval on 29 Apr 2025 (1 Dhul-Qi'dah 1446), published Umm Al-Qura 12 May 2025 (14 Dhul-Qi'dah 1446), and renamed "نظام رسوم الأراضي البيضاء والعقارات الشاغرة" (White Land and Vacant Property Fees); amended art. 3: (1) annual fee of up to 10% of land value (was 2.5%), state property excluded; (2) the Minister sets the application scope and the area, with the parcel or the aggregate holding not below 5,000 m² (was 10,000 m²) — white-land exec. reg. art. 7(4) applies the 5,000 m² floor to one owner's aggregate vacant land within the city; (3) vacant buildings pay an annual fee as a share of fair rent (أجرة المثل) capped at 5% of property value, which the Council of Ministers may raise to 10% on the ministerial committee's proposal. White-land executive regulation, Umm Al-Qura 28/2/1447H = 22 Aug 2025: five bands 10% / 7.5% / 5% / 2.5% / nil, with each city's zones and rates set by ministerial decision (art. 6). Vacant-property executive regulation now ISSUED (was due within one year of the 12 May 2025 publication): ministerial decision 4700822503/1 of 26/11/1447H, Umm Al-Qura 28/11/1447H = 15 May 2026, in force on publication; art. 6(1)(a) settles the fee base — a share of fair rent, not above 5% of building value — so the share-of-rent vs share-of-value question is closed; a building counts as vacant after 6 months' non-use, continuous or cumulative, in the reference year (art. 7 per a full-text reproduction and MOMAH's FAQ); the fee applies only in zones the Minister announces after at least 12 months of monitoring vacancy and market criteria (arts 5, 9); MOMAH's FAQ (read 2026-10-21) says covered cities and zones will be announced later — no vacant-property zone announcement located as of 2026-10-21, verify at MOMAH; TRANSFER GATE: Council of Ministers Decision 263 of 12/3/1448H (Umm Al-Qura 22/3/1448H = 4 Sep 2026) bars documenting any real-estate disposal that transfers ownership of land subject to the law until payment of the fees due is verified, and requires non-transfer dispositions (e.g. mortgage) to be documented so as to protect the state's right to collect the fees — verified 2026-10-21 | https://laws.boe.gov.sa/BoeLaws/Laws/LawDetails/6932e745-0f61-4f25-9e4a-a9a700f21d6b/1 / https://momah.gov.sa/ar/node/14963 / https://momah.gov.sa/ar/node/14971 / https://www.uqn.gov.sa/details?p=27369 / https://www.uqn.gov.sa/decisions-and-regulations/4000949 / https://www.uqn.gov.sa/decisions-and-regulations/4000950 / https://idlelands.momah.gov.sa/ar/guide/86011 / https://www.uqn.gov.sa/decisions-and-regulations/4001765 | 2026-10-21 | 2027-01-19 | 1 | --tax --price --compare --exit (SA)`

- `2026-10-19 (signed; not in force) | other | Andorra–EU Association Agreement — SIGNED 19 Oct 2026 in Brussels (European Commission for the EU, Head of Government Xavier Espot for Andorra, alongside San Marino — Commission IP/26/2182 and Govern AA portal news item 19 Oct 2026); updated euro Monetary Agreements were signed the same day. The Council of the EU authorised signature AND provisional application on 16 Jul 2026, and its release calls the text a mixed agreement (pre-signing by the member states). Next steps: European Parliament consent (EP Legislative Observatory procedure 2024/0101(NLE), checked 2026-10-28: 'Preparatory phase in Parliament', 'Indicative plenary sitting date' 18 Jan 2027, a forecast, not a scheduled vote), then conclusion by the Council; in Andorra a consultative referendum under Constitution art. 76 after EP consent (the Head of Government has committed to treat the result as binding), with the text sent to the Consell General only if the yes vote wins, where it needs a qualified majority of two thirds of the consellers generals; the Govern says the timetable will take account of the EU institutions' procedures and the Andorran general elections expected in spring 2027 (Govern d'Andorra, 19 Oct 2026). NOT IN FORCE and NOT provisionally applied as at 2026-10-28 — the Govern states the signature brings neither entry into force nor provisional application, and the Commission says the Agreement will be ready for provisional application as soon as the ratification procedures in Andorra and San Marino allow; the adopted Council decision on signature and provisional application is not yet in the Official Journal (EU Publications Office index searched 2026-10-28). The Govern FAQ still lists a Council decision on the agreement's legal nature (mixed or not) as a pending step, while the Council release (EU primary) already calls it mixed; referendum date not set (the AA portal's latest news item is the 19 Oct 2026 release, checked 2026-10-28) | https://ec.europa.eu/commission/presscorner/detail/en/ip_26_2182 + https://www.consilium.europa.eu/en/press/press-releases/2026/07/16/council-greenlights-eu-deal-with-andorra-and-san-marino + https://andorraue.ad/ca/acord-d-associacio + https://andorraue.ad/ca/actualitat/actualitat/2026/10/19/143/andorra-signa-l-acord-d-associacio-amb-la-unio-europea-i-obre-la-fase-de-ratificacio/ + https://oeil.europarl.europa.eu/oeil/en/procedure-file?reference=2024/0101(NLE) | 2026-10-28 | 2027-01-19 | 2 | --macro --tax --visa (AD)`

No.'; Council negotiating mandate Decision (EU) 2024/1693 of 30 May 2024, OJ L 14.6.2024): negotiations concluded 26 Jan 2026, initialled 25 Mar 2026 per COM(2026) 445 (the Govern d'Andorra's 9 Sep 2026 release says 30 Mar); on 7 Sep 2026 the Commission proposed signature and provisional application (COM(2026) 445, 2026/0248(NLE)) and conclusion after EP consent (COM(2026) 444, 2026/0247(NLE)). Same content as the SM agreement: no border checks with France and Spain (art. 11); Andorran residence permits give 90/180-day visa-free Schengen access with EES and ETIAS exemption (arts. 21–22); a third-country national's permit is issued or renewed only after a French or Spanish security check, 28 days extendable to 42 (art. 25(5)–(6)), and only on a 'genuine connection' — actual and regular physical presence; 'predetermined investments having been made in the Principality of Andorra's economy or real estate' do not count (art. 24(2)); permits issued before it applies stay valid at most two years (art. 26(1)) — the Govern FAQ says every residence card must be replaced within the first two years and passive or investment residence 's'ha de basar en una vinculació real i efectiva amb Andorra. No n'hi ha prou amb una inversió econòmica o un pagament administratiu per si sols' (must rest on a real, effective link; an investment or administrative payment alone is not enough). As of 2026-10-28: no Council signing decision in the OJ (Publications Office SPARQL, index current to 28 Oct 2026); EP file at 'Preparatory phase in Parliament', only event 07/09/2026 (OEIL). Current law (NTS): 종부세 housing base = (aggregate 공시가격 − ₩900M, or ₩1.2B for 1세대1주택) × 60%; rates 0.5–2.7% for up to 2 homes and 0.5–5.0% for 3+ homes (the 조정대상지역 2-home surcharge rate was abolished from 2023); the 양도세 조정대상지역 multi-home surcharge (+20%p for 2 homes, +30%p for 3+, no long-term holding deduction) applies again since 10 May 2026, top effective rate 82.5% incl. local tax. PROPOSED: 종부세 rates move from a house-count to a house-value schedule from 2028; the 1세대1주택 holding-period 세액공제 becomes a residence-period credit (5+ years); 공정시장가액비율 60% → 70% in 2027, then from 2028 70% for 1세대1주택 and 1–2 homes outside the capital region and 80% for 3+ homes and other 조정대상지역 multi-home holders; basic deductions — resident single home ₩1.2B → ₩1.4B, non-resident single home stays ₩1.2B (the 3 Aug cut to ₩900M was withdrawn on 1 Sep), non-resident joint-owner couples ₩900M each → ₩600M each, other holdings ₩900M → ₩400M + ₩500M × resident-home share (all four deduction figures as in bill 2221046); tax-burden cap stays 150% (the rise to 200% was withdrawn on 1 Sep); 양도세 surcharge easing for 조정대상지역 homes held at least 2 years on sales from 1 Jan 2027 — 2 homes +5%p (2027) / +10%p (2028), 3+ homes +10%p / +15%p, reverting to +20/+30%p from 2029, and surcharge-paid sales from 10 May 2026 resettled at the 2027 rates at the 2027 interim or May 2027 final filing; the long-term holding deduction for homes becomes residence-based (1주택 8%/yr, max 80%, from 2029; capped per taxpayer and per home at ₩2B in 2028 and ₩1B from 2029, bill 2221042); 양도세 basic deduction ₩2.5M → ₩25M for a single home of 10+ years' residence sold for ₩3B or less. IN FORCE since 1 Oct 2026 (소득세법 시행령 대통령령 제36737호 and 종합부동산세법 시행령 대통령령 제36739호, promulgated 30 Sep 2026, 부칙 제1조; law.go.kr): the 조정대상지역 일시적 2주택 window for 양도세 and 종부세 single-home relief is cut from 3 years to 2 (homes acquired, or contracted with a deposit paid, by 3 Aug 2026 keep 3 years); the 양도세 surcharge exclusion for registered purchased-rental (민간매입임대) apartments in 조정대상지역 applies only to sales by 31 Dec 2027 (later where the mandatory rental term ends from 2027, the area is newly designated from 2027, or a redevelopment approval comes first) | 재정경제부 `https://www.moef.go.kr/nw/mosfnw/detailInfograpView.do?searchNttId1=MOSF_000000000078819&menuNo=4040500` + Samil PwC `https://www.pwc.com/kr/ko/insights/tax-news-flash/samilpwc_tax-news-flash_260803_kr.pdf` (secondary) + Newspim `https://www.newspim.com/news/view/20260901001132` (secondary) + Hankyoreh `https://www.hani.co.kr/arti/economy/economy_general/1275752.html` (secondary) + likms `https://likms.assembly.go.kr/bill/bi/billDetailPage.do?billId=ARC_O2S6R0Z9Z0P3V1Z0A5J3R1Y8B6L3L6` + `https://likms.assembly.go.kr/bill/bi/billDetailPage.do?billId=ARC_B2K6R0N9D0U3I1I0N3B1C5L0K3O7L4` + law.go.kr `https://www.law.go.kr/법령/소득세법시행령` + `https://www.law.go.kr/법령/종합부동산세법시행령` | 2026-10-28 | 2026-12-10 | 2 | --tax --exit (KR)`

## EU-wide instruments — transposition and application dates

A **directive** binds all 27 member states but reaches a buyer only through national law; the transposition deadline is the date by which that national law must exist. A **regulation** applies directly, so its application date is the date it bites. Every row below was read off the instrument's own EUR-Lex text on the verified date — do not restate a deadline from memory.

| Instrument | Topic | Deadline / application | Status (verified 2026-07-31) | Affected sections |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **(EU) 2024/1275** (EPBD recast, directive) | Energy performance — zero-emission buildings, MEPS, mandatory renovation passports | Transposition **29 May 2026**; art. 17(15) (no financial incentives for stand-alone fossil-fuel boilers) was due earlier, **1 Jan 2025** | **Deadline passed, unmet everywhere** — on 15 Jul 2026 the Commission opened infringement procedures with letters of formal notice to **all 27** member states for failing to fully transpose; two months to respond, then a possible reasoned opinion ([DG ENER, 15 Jul 2026](https://energy.ec.europa.eu/news/commission-calls-eu-countries-transpose-reinforced-rules-energy-performance-buildings-2026-07-15_en)). The October 2026 package issued no reasoned opinion on transposition; it sent reasoned opinions to BE, CZ, IE, IT, LU, HU, MT, PL and SK for not submitting their draft National Building Renovation Plans, due 31 Dec 2025 (letters of formal notice Mar 2026) ([DG ENER, 1 Oct 2026](https://energy.ec.europa.eu/news/october-infringements-package-key-decisions-energy-2026-10-01_en); checked 2026-10-21) | `--esg` `--rental` |
| **(EU) 2024/1028** (regulation) | Short-let data collection + registration harmonisation | Directly applicable, **applies 20 May 2026** — a single date, no later tranche; member states had to adopt the penalty provisions by the same date; Commission evaluation due by 20 May 2031 (art. 18) | In application since 20 May 2026 | `--rental` |
| **(EU) 2024/1640** (AMLD6, directive) | Beneficial-ownership registers + access rules; **art. 18 single access point to real-estate information** | Main transposition **10 Jul 2027**. Derogations (art. 78): art. 74 by 10 Jul 2025 · **arts. 11, 12, 13 and 15 — BO-register access, incl. legitimate-interest access — by 10 Jul 2026** · **art. 18 by 10 Jul 2029** | 10 Jul 2026 access tranche deadline passed — on 25 Sep 2026 the Commission sent letters of formal notice to 18 member states (BE, BG, CZ, DE, EE, EL, ES, FR, HR, CY, LT, LU, NL, AT, PL, PT, RO, FI) for not fully notifying arts. 11, 12, 13 and 15 ([INF/26/1834](https://ec.europa.eu/commission/presscorner/detail/en/inf_26_1834), re-verified 2026-10-23); EE: the Ministry of Finance postponed its switch on 10 Jul 2026 and BO data were still public on 2026-10-21 (fin.ee; see shared/sanctions.md); main deadline pending | `--tax` (ownership) `--finance` |
| **(EU) 2023/2226** (DAC8, Council directive) | Crypto-asset reporting | Adopt + publish by **31 Dec 2025**, apply from **1 Jan 2026**; two later tranches — 31 Dec 2027 (apply 1 Jan 2028) and 31 Dec 2029 (apply 1 Jan 2030) | First tranche in application since 1 Jan 2026; per-country transposition not individually verified | `--tax` |
| **(EU) 2024/1689** (AI Act, regulation) | High-risk AI — incl. systems that **evaluate creditworthiness or establish a credit score** of natural persons (annex III, 5(b)), i.e. mortgage decisioning | Directly applicable. General application **2 Aug 2026**; chapters I–II from 2 Feb 2025; chapter III section 4, V, VII, XII + art. 78 from 2 Aug 2025; art. 6(1) (the annex I product-safety route) deferred to 2 Aug 2027. Annex III systems are high-risk under **art. 6(2)**, so they follow the general date | Annex III high-risk obligations apply from 2 Aug 2026 | `--finance` |
| **(EU) 2023/1791** (EED recast, directive) | Energy efficiency | Transposition **11 Oct 2025**; applies from 12 Oct 2025 (art. 37 from 30 Jun 2024) | Deadline passed. **Supersedes Directive 2012/27/EU** — which Directive (EU) 2018/2002 amended — repealed with effect from 12 Oct 2025 (art. 39); cite the recast, never 2018/2002 | `--esg` |
| **(EU) 2026/849** (CRR RTS, delegated) | What counts as an **equivalent legal mechanism** — i.e. a completion guarantee — for a residential property **under construction** under CRR art. 124(3)(a)(iii)(2); decides whether a lender may apply the preferential immovable-property risk weight to an off-plan mortgage | n/a — directly applicable, no transposition; **applies 12 Aug 2026** | Adopted 16 Apr 2026 (C/2026/2429), [OJ L, 2026/849, 23.7.2026](https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/reg_del/2026/849/oj/eng); in force 20th day after publication. Built on EBA final draft EBA/RTS/2025/05 (submitted 5 Aug 2025) + EBA opinion EBA/Op/2026/03 (20 Feb 2026). Verified 2026-07-31 | `--finance` `--type=off-plan` |
| **(EU) 2026/1386** (FDI screening, regulation; repeals 2019/452) | Screening of foreign investments — a **corporate** regime: an investment by a non-EU national or third-country undertaking in a "Union target" (an undertaking under a member state's law) that gives effective participation in its management or control (art. 2(1), (5), (9)); portfolio investments excluded (recital 14); no numeric filing thresholds (member states may set them, recital 20) | Directly applicable, no transposition, but each member state must establish a screening mechanism that complies with it (art. 3(1)). In force 16 Jul 2026; **applies 17 Jan 2028** (art. 31; arts. 3(2), 15(2), 18(1)–(6), 18(9)–(12) and 27–29 from 16 Jul 2026); member states notify their screening mechanisms by 17 Jan 2028 (art. 3(2)) | Adopted 17 Jun 2026, [OJ L, 2026/1386, 26.6.2026](https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/reg/2026/1386/oj/eng); text read 2026-10-28 via the EU Publications Office Cellar (EUR-Lex returned an empty bot check); the only corrigendum (3 Sep 2026) is Polish-language. Mandatory prior authorisation (art. 4(15)) covers dual-use and military items, semiconductors, quantum and AI, critical transport, energy and digital infrastructure, strategic raw materials, systemic financial entities and election systems — **not real estate or farmland as such**. Land and property needed to operate critical infrastructure (art. 19(1)(c)), farming where the target "possesses or operates more than 10 000 ha of farmland" (art. 19(1)(i)) and proximity to military or sensitive sites (art. 19(1)(j)) are **assessment factors** for investments a national mechanism covers; a member state may widen that scope (art. 4(16)). A private home purchase by an individual involves no Union target, so it is outside; a non-EU buyer taking control of a property-holding company that carries out an economic activity can be a foreign investment (art. 2(1)), screened where the national mechanism covers it. The Commission's 23 Oct 2026 answer to E-003241/2026 gives "applicable as of July 2028" — art. 31 says 17 Jan 2028; cite the OJ | `--tax` (ownership) |

**Rule**: For any EU country playbook's `--esg` or `--rental` section, EPBD recast 2024/1275 must be referenced if the section claims any energy-rating rental ban schedule. Since 15 Jul 2026 **no member state has fully transposed it** (all 27 hold a letter of formal notice), so never present an EPBD requirement as if it already bound an owner in a given country — cite the *national* law that exists today, and name the directive only as the source of the obligation to legislate. A national rental-ban schedule that predates the recast (e.g. the FR DPE ladder) stands on its own national footing and is unaffected by this.

**Rule**: The AI Act's high-risk obligations for creditworthiness/credit-scoring systems (annex III, 5(b) → art. 6(2)) apply from **2 Aug 2026**. They bind the *provider and deployer* of the scoring system — a bank, not the buyer — so a `--finance` section may note that a mortgage decision made by an automated system now carries transparency and human-oversight duties, but must not imply the buyer gains a direct claim or that a specific lender is non-compliant.

**Rule**: Reg (EU) 2026/849 is a **bank-side prudential rule, not a buyer obligation** — never present it as something the buyer must satisfy. It is relevant only where a `--type=off-plan` or `--finance` brief discusses mortgage availability: art. 1(2) requires the completion guarantee to be one the **member state's own law requires**, so a jurisdiction with no statutory completion guarantee cannot meet the test at all. Whether a given national scheme (FR *garantie financière d'achèvement*, ES *aval bancario* / *seguro de caución*, IT *polizza fideiussoria*, …) satisfies **all** of art. 1(2)–(13) is the lender's assessment — do not assert that it does.

## Watchlist — rumored / proposed (Tier 4)

Things signalled but not enacted. Track for upgrade to Tier 1–3 when official.

| Country | Signal | Source signal | Last heard | Re-check |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 🇦🇱 AL | DIVA STR registration system Jan 2026 launch | Albanian government press | 2026-01 | 2026-07 |
| 🇪🇸 ES | National 100% gravamen on non-EU non-resident buyers — **LAPSED (caducado) 6 Oct 2026**. PSOE Proposición de Ley 122/000196 (BOCG B-229-1, 30/05/2025) registered 22 May 2025, at "Pleno - Toma en consideración" from 5 Sep 2025 to 6 Oct 2026, **never voted**; "Concluido - (Caducado)" since 6 Oct 2026, when Real Decreto 806/2026 (BOE núm. 248, 6 Oct 2026) dissolved the Cortes for the 29 Nov 2026 general election; dropped from Jan 2026 housing package; Reuters 27/03/2026 confirmed no parliamentary debate; the 6 Oct 2026 housing RDLs carry no non-EU buyer tax (La Moncloa, 8 Oct 2026); no BOE-published Ley. A revival needs a new initiative in the next legislature — watch the post-election government. | 🇪🇺 EU | **Affordable Housing Act** — COM(2026) 599 final, procedure 2026/0268(COD), Commission proposal for a Regulation (Art. 114 TFEU) published 9 Sep 2026, first reading (committee referral announced in Parliament 22 Oct 2026; IMCO lead with no rapporteur appointed as at 2026-10-28; EMPL, TRAN and REGI for opinion, EMPL rapporteur for opinion appointed 15 Oct 2026): a framework letting member states restrict (a) short-term rental services and (b) acquisition or use of residential property NOT used as a primary residence, gated on three cumulative art. 6(1) criteria — price-to-income ratio ≥ 8 (a), that ratio having risen over the latest 10 years of data (b), stress unlikely to ease within three years (c) — with (b) waived where the ratio is ≥ 10 (art. 6(2)); acquisition restrictions apply only to property acquired after the measure (art. 10(1)(f)); measures capped at 5 years renewable (art. 12); measures adopted before entry into force exempt (art. 14); art. 3(1) drops the Services Directive art. 15(7) notification for STR measures. NOT adopted, no entry-into-force date — never present as binding; placed on this watchlist rather than a tier-3 entry because it is a proposal with no legal effect yet. Related: EP written question E-003241/2026 on non-EU purchases in cross-border areas, answered 23 Oct 2026: no dedicated horizontal EU framework governs real-estate acquisition, so oversight incl. source-of-capital checks is largely national law; the answer cites FDI-screening Regulation (EU) 2026/1386 (per the answer, applicable July 2028 and screening corporate transactions incl. asset deals that raise security or public-order risks, inter alia on land and property needed by a critical entity or critical infrastructure and farmland over 10,000 ha; the OJ text applies it from 17 Jan 2028, art. 31 — see the EU-wide instruments row) and AMLR 2024/1624 art. 67 (non-EU legal entities and legal arrangements acquiring EU real estate file beneficial-ownership information with that member state's central register) — AMLR per the answer, not read on EUR-Lex | EUR-Lex CELEX 52026PC0599 + EP Legislative Observatory https://oeil.europarl.europa.eu/oeil/en/procedure-file?reference=2026/0268(COD) + E-003241/2026 answer https://data.europarl.europa.eu/api/v2/parliamentary-questions/E-10-2026-003241 | 2026-10 | 2027-03 |

Procedural — imposes nothing on buyers; the 2027 permitting / renovation package is the `--permits` / `--renovation` watch item | europarl.europa.eu TA-10-2026-0234 | 2026-09 | 2027-03 |

Opinion not binding; judgment pending; no property / tax / STR effect — `--visa` family-reunification limb only | curia.europa.eu cp260129en.pdf | 2026-09 | 2027-03 |
| 🇪🇺 EU | **PSD3 + Payment Services Regulation (PSR)** — procedures 2023/0209(COD) and 2023/0210(COD): political agreement 27 Nov 2025; the Council published the final compromise texts 23 Apr 2026 and EP ECON approved them 5 May 2026 (PE787.673 / PE787.675); both files read 'Awaiting Council's 1st reading position', indicative EP plenary 14 Dec 2026; NOT adopted and no Official Journal reference as at 2026-10-21. Per the final texts (Clifford Chance briefing, 28 Apr 2026, secondary): entry into force 20 days after OJ publication; PSD3 transposition and most PSR rules 21 months after entry into force; PSR verification of payee and its liability rules 27 months — est. late 2028 for general application if OJ publication lands in early 2027 (computed: OJ + 20 days + 21 months). Until then PSD2, incl. its D+1 execution rule, governs. Placed on this watchlist rather than a tier-3 entry because it has no legal effect yet; `--remote` / `--finance` | EP Legislative Observatory https://oeil.europarl.europa.eu/oeil/en/procedure-file?reference=2023/0209(COD) + https://oeil.europarl.europa.eu/oeil/en/procedure-file?reference=2023/0210(COD) | 2026-10 | 2027-01 |

## Per-playbook impact map

When a Tier 1–2 entry is added here, these playbook sections need re-stamping:

| Topic | Sections to re-stamp |
|---|---|
| tax (rate/threshold) | `--tax`, `--rental` if rental-affecting, country playbook benchmark tables |
| visa (open/close) | `--visa`, country playbook `--work` if tied, `shared/visa-programs.md` |
| rental (regulation) | `--rental`, `--tax` if cedolare-style regime |
| esg (energy-rating) | `--esg`, `--rental` (rental-ban schedules) |
| ownership (foreign buyer) | country playbook `--tax` ownership block, `shared/exit.md` foreign-buyer-pool table |
| eurozone | `--currency`, `--tax`, all country benchmarks in local currency |
| cadastre | country playbook `--mains` + `--tax`, `shared/comparable-transactions.md` tier classification |
| pension regime | `shared/retirement.md`, country playbook `--tax` |

---

## Maintenance cadence

This file gets the same auto-validate treatment as country playbooks:

- **Weekly**: URL liveness on every entry (cron via `auto-validate.md`)
- **Monthly**: scan all entries with `revisit_by` ≤ 30 days from today; create review queue
- **Quarterly**: full re-research of all Tier 1 entries (visa programs especially); confirm ENDED programs haven't been resurrected
- **At-event**: when a regulatory news item lands (EU Official Journal, national gazette, ECJ ruling, central-bank decision), add or update an entry **before** it propagates errors into playbooks

Subscribe these feeds for at-event updates:
- EU Official Journal: https://eur-lex.europa.eu/oj/direct-access.html
- ECJ press releases: https://curia.europa.eu/juris/recherche.jsf?language=en
- Per-country gazettes (LégiFrance, Gazzetta Ufficiale, BOE, etc. — already in country playbooks)
- OECD Tax Policy Reforms annual report
- Tax Foundation Europe weekly digest

---

## Format for new entries

```markdown
- `<effective_date> | <topic> | <one-line summary> | <source name> | <verified_date> | <revisit_by> | <tier> | <sections>`
```

Multi-line is fine if the entry is complex; the seven-pipe format is the canonical machine-readable form.

## Related files

- `shared/anti-hallucination.md` — defines the confidence-label tiers this file's `revisit_by` feeds into
- `shared/updater.md` — auto-downgrade rule that uses this file's `revisit_by` to decay confidence
- `shared/visa-programs.md` — universal `--visa` section; ENDED registry above is the source of truth
- `shared/auto-validate.md` — cron infrastructure that re-checks URLs in this file weekly
- `shared/exit.md` — foreign-buyer-pool tables that depend on Tier 1 ownership entries
